tbmm-2020-07-08sit03spe0236par0253	Congressmans, I'd like to briefly explain the opinion of our presidency regarding the opposition to the Constitutional Opposition of the Proposal. As it is known, according to Article 38 of the Civil Code, "Commissions are obliged to first examine whether the proposals referred to them are against the text and spirit of the Constitution." The Commission must reject it before it meets the terms of the proposal it considers unconstitutional. The Justice Commission did not view the proposal as unconstitutional, so it made its report to our presidency and took its place on our proposal agenda. It is possible for the General Assembly to present claims of unconstitutionality during talks on the entire proposal. After these talks, the General Assembly has the authority to seriously deny the adoption of claims against the Constitution. Again, according to Article 84 of the Civil Code, motions for rejection are voted before other motions on the grounds that a specific clause of the proposal is contrary to the Constitution during the meeting of the General Assembly. As you can see, there are many rules that allow the proposal to be rejected either on the commission or on the General Assembly if it is considered unconstitutional. For these reasons, I feel that it is not in accordance with the Civil Code to discuss claims of violation of the Constitution before starting negotiations with the proposal. On the grounds that I have explained, there is no change in my position to start negotiations with the proposal.

According to this, if there are more than one bar in one province, the legal aid office will be created on equal representation of the bar. Bars will be represented equally in the administration, regardless of the number of members in the legal aid office, but the principle of equality among lawyers will be met with a fair balance if lawyers receive work at the legal aid offices and files. The procedure and the basis for the tasks will be determined by regulations, which will be adopted by the Turkish Bar Association, and the Ministry of Justice will approve.
